### F-309 — the reranker validates a graph contract the exporter wrote about itself, and the measured evidence that could contradict it is not shipped or loadable

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-E1-04`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN
- Root cause: the runtime check compares the manifest's graph-contract values against the same literals the export script stamps into that manifest, rather than against properties measured from the loaded session, so the guard can only ever agree with the file it trusts. The genuinely measured artefacts — the fidelity report, the exporter and quantiser records, and the digest of the fidelity evidence — are either excluded from the shipped file set or absent from the manifest dataclass, so they are dropped at load and nothing downstream can re-verify them.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/reranker.py:131-141` — `contract.get("max_batch_size") != 20`, `max_sequence_length` against the module constant, `one_logit_per_pair is not True`, and a CPU-provider equality check · Where those numbers are authored: `apps/knowledge/scripts/export_reranker.py:219` writes `graph_contract` as the literal batch axis 0, sequence axis 1, max batch 20, max sequence 1024, one logit per pair true, alongside `exporter`, `quantizer` and `fidelity_evidence_sha256` · Where the shipped set is decided: `:212` runtime paths cover the int8 model, its external data files and the tokenizer only, and `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:108` copies only the runtime-artifacts directory · Where the fields are lost: `apps/knowledge/app/reranker.py:29-38` the manifest dataclass has no exporter, quantiser or fidelity member, and `:80-90` the loader reads only the ten fields it has, so the extra keys are discarded at load · Counter-evidence measured this session so the row is not overstated: `:211` pins the tokenizer digest as a literal, but `reranker.py:118-125` re-hashes the deployed tokenizer file, so that pin is genuinely checked. · measured at HEAD: `ArtifactManifest` has nine fields (`reranker.py:30-38`), not ten, and the exporter path is `apps/knowledge/scripts/export_reranker.py:205-206`. · R7-H (vị trí mới, gộp): vòng tròn provenance mở rộng sang cả module hợp đồng — `apps/knowledge/app/reranker_contract.py:1-3` là nơi duy nhất declares `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3`, revision `953dc6f6…` và `SOURCE_MODEL_SHA256 = d9e3e081…`; `reranker.py:96-98` kiểm manifest bằng đúng ba hằng đó; `apps/knowledge/scripts/export_reranker.py:19-23` sinh manifest CŨNG từ ba hằng đó; và `apps/knowledge/tests/test_reranker.py:14-18` import lại chúng (đo: 0 lần chuỗi literal `BAAI/`, `953dc6f6`, `d9e3e081` xuất hiện trong file test). Pin độc lập duy nhất là `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:64-66` (`ADD --checksum=sha256:d9e3e081…` trên URL chứa revision), nhưng không lane enforced nào chạy `docker build` — `.github/workflows/ci.yml:43` chạy `npm run verify`, `:100` chạy `npm run test:knowledge`. Đổi cả ba hằng số thì mọi lane vẫn xanh; mismatch chi được phát hiện ở một lần build image thủ công.
- Reproduction: static - hand-edit a fixture manifest so its graph_contract repeats the constants while the deployed session actually caps batches or sequence lengths elsewhere (a dynamic-axis graph carries no max): the two ceilings and the one-logit flag are never exercised against the session, and the only measurement that bounded them - the 1-by-8, 20-by-17 and 1-by-1024 probe recorded in `apps/knowledge/scripts/export_reranker.py:105-127` and written to the fidelity file at `:189` - is neither copied into the runtime set (`:216-217`) nor shipped (`apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:108`) nor loadable (`apps/knowledge/app/reranker.py:29-38`, `:80-90`). Note the guard is not purely tautological: input shapes, axis dynamism, output arity and provider are measured from the session at `:137-177`.
- Acceptance criteria: `graph_contract` values are computed from the exported session rather than typed, and the fidelity evidence digest is either shipped and re-verified at load or removed from the manifest together with the export step that produces it.
- Related findings: F-175, F-252, F-312 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E1 — Knowledgebase python modules

### F-315 — the migration-digest guard reconciles only manifest-to-disk, and its own fixture test mandates the blind direction

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-E3-03`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Gates + CI, governance records
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — escalated to the owner on 2026-10-20 (M12/T9): how a new migration enters the pinned migration-digest manifest. The agent stopped at the safest boundary (AGENTS.md §7); this row stays in the queue until the owner decides.
- Root cause: the guard walks the entries named in the digest manifest and hashes the file each one points at, so it can detect a listed file changing but never notices a migration file that is absent from the manifest; the manifest has no other reader in the repository, and the suite that owns this invariant asserts as desired behaviour that a new unlisted migration reddens nothing. The consequence is that the control is one-directional by design and the blind direction is pinned green, so the only way to close it is to add the reverse enumeration rather than to weaken anything.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m12-t2.2-a4-migration-digest.test.mjs:64` iterates `manifest.migrations` and `:123-132` is the only real gate, hashing disk contents for those entries · Where: `:201-213` a fixture writes a fourth migration file and asserts the failure list stays empty, titled as the forward-migration allowance · Where the manifest is consumed: a grep for its filename returns this suite, the contracts document itself and governance notes — no loader in the application · Where the adoption key lives: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py` applies migrations by filename and records the version row, which is the mechanism F-166 records as unreappliable after a lost row · Where the sibling gap is recorded: F-221 notes that only one of the three files is exercised by a real lane.
- Reproduction: static mutation probe — remove one entry from the manifest and rewrite that SQL file freely: both real assertions still pass, because nothing asks whether every file on disk is listed. The fixer test: a case requiring every tracked migration file to appear in the manifest, with a non-empty failure list when one is dropped.
- Acceptance criteria: the guard reconciles in both directions (every listed entry matches disk, and every disk file is listed), and the forward-migration allowance is expressed as an explicit owner decision about how a new file enters the manifest rather than as an absence of checking.
- Related findings: F-166, F-221, F-218 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E3 — JS test validity, first half

### F-317 — a file-wide occurrence count is asserted as per-list membership, with a failure message describing a condition the assertion cannot evaluate

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-E3-06`
- Affected modules: Workflow topology + ledger, Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN
- Root cause: the assertion splits a source file on a marker and requires the count to be two, with a message stating the entry must appear in the preflight list and the apply list. A count cannot bind membership to either list: two copies inside one window satisfy it while the other window is empty. The row is graded Low rather than Medium because a sibling suite does bind the same structure positionally and would catch the regression; what this assertion adds is a reword-sensitive gate, not coverage.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m10-t2-r8-observability-predecessor.test.mjs:99-111` the count comparison and the preflight-versus-apply failure message, and the same shape at `:113-130`, whose membership check is a file-wide includes plus a split count · Where the two real lists are: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1286-1530` the preflight predecessor array and `:1631-1797` the apply array, with the candidate digest at `:1359` and `:1731` and the baseline digest at `:1472` and `:1703` · Measured: re-running the sibling's window-slice predicate in node prints preflight 1286-1530 with 40 digests and apply 1631-1797 with 40 digests, each R8 digest present once per window and no digest missing from apply · The structural sibling that does bind: `tests/m8-t2.10-r13-r7-predecessor-allowlist.test.mjs:867-900`, while its own count case at `:850-864` shares the weak idiom. · corrected by the R7-E challenge pass (measured at HEAD): the two real lists are `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1280-1531` (array literal 1286-1530, R5 digest at `:1472`) and `:1625-1798` (`materializeOwnedFile`, array 1631-1797, R5 digest at `:1703`); measured occurrences are `0fb628e8` × 2 (`:1472`, `:1703`) and `6a7968b7` × 2 (`:1359`, `:1731`). The sibling `tests/m8-t2.10-r13-r7-predecessor-allowlist.test.mjs:867-900` binds preflight ⊆ apply in one direction only.
- Reproduction: static — place both entries in the preflight window and empty the apply window: the count stays two and the assertion passes. The fixer change: use the sibling's two-window slice and assert set containment per window, so the case is falsifiable in exactly the way the message describes.
- Acceptance criteria: the assertion evaluates membership per window rather than a file-wide count, and both-entries-in-one-window reddens it.
- Related findings: F-218, F-213 (the same file's historical-object dependency), F-214 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E3 — JS test validity, first half

### F-323 — the byte-identical acceptance pair is pinned only against each other, and one driver pins the embedding model while the other inherits the operator's

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-E5-04`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Zink Knowledgebase, Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — mechanism reproduced by execution on 2026-10-19 (owner's `b1-call`): two byte-identical acceptance overlays resolve two different embedding models while the dimension assertion stays fixed. It stays LATENT because the live host is masked today for a reason now measured rather than assumed: the operator env omits the key, so the compose default answers both drivers with the same value.
- Root cause: the two acceptance overlays are held identical by a text-equality test, which is the only cross-check between them, and the divergence that matters sits outside the compared bytes: the third driver sets the embedding model in the shell before composing, the fourth leaves it to the env file, and neither overlay carries the value. Two acceptance runs can therefore resolve different embedding models while their compose files are provably identical, and the byte gate cannot see it because it compares files, not resolutions.
- Evidence: Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t3.ps1:20` saves the prior value, `:100` assigns the model explicitly and `:175-176` restores it · Measured: a case-insensitive grep for that variable across `infrastructure/docker` returns the assignment in the third driver only, and the save-and-restore lines are the third driver's alone — the fifth driver's cleanup at `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t5.ps1:283-285` clears the run id, the acceptance vault root and the image revision · Where the substitution defaults: `infrastructure/docker/compose/compose.core.yml:150` with dimensions fixed at `:149`, and every driver composes through an explicit env file at `knowledge-t3.ps1:24`, `knowledge-t4.ps1:23` and `knowledge-t5.ps1:33`, so the fourth driver resolves the value from the operator env file while the third overrides it in the shell · Where the byte check lives: `tests/m12-t2.2-b15-compose-equivalence.test.mjs:33-43`, and a grep for the two overlay names across the tests directory returns that file only · Measured: the two overlays compare byte-identical at HEAD and that lane prints tests 2, pass 2, fail 0 · Why the dimensions matter: the fourth driver builds 1024-element probe vectors at `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t4.ps1:54` and `:235` and asserts the hnsw index at `:55-56` and `:86-87`, while the first driver asserts the 1024 length at `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t1.ps1:128` and the fifth at `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t5-common.ps1:327` and `:342` — a wrong-dimension model is caught by three acceptance runs, not by one. · executed 2026-10-19: `docker compose --profile knowledge --env-file <scratch env setting KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_MODEL=nomic-embed-text:v1.5> -f compose.core.yml -f compose.m3-t4.acceptance.yml config` renders `KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_MODEL: nomic-embed-text:v1.5`, while the same render with the shell assignment `knowledge-t3.ps1:100` performs (`bge-m3:latest`) resolves `KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_MODEL: bge-m3:latest` — and **both** keep `KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS: "1024"`, so the two acceptance runs disagree on the model while the dimension gate stays green. · measured: `sha256sum` of the two acceptance overlays is identical (`e61ad1c23cd6…` for both) — precisely what the byte gate at `tests/m12-t2.2-b15-compose-equivalence.test.mjs:33-43` can see; nothing can see a resolution difference. · why still LATENT on this host: `infrastructure/docker/env/.env` carries no `KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_MODEL` key at all (only the key's presence was checked, no values read), so the `${KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_MODEL:-bge-m3:latest}` default at `compose.core.yml:150` answers both drivers identically today; the divergence fires as soon as an operator env sets the key.
- Reproduction: static — nothing in the enforced lanes resolves the composed environment, and the operator env file was deliberately not opened under this round's rules, so whether the current host carries an overriding value is unmeasured. The fixer measurement: compose the fourth driver's stack and print the resolved embedding model next to the dimension the first and fifth drivers assert.
- Acceptance criteria: either both drivers pin the model the dimensions were designed for, or the overlays carry it explicitly, and one lane asserts the resolved value rather than only the file bytes. This does not ask to widen the byte-equality test, which is doing its stated job.
- Related findings: F-288, F-262, F-223 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E5 — remaining orphan files

### F-324 — the router placeholder convention is enforced in a helper that six suites bypass, and one of them spawns a generator with the inherited environment

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R7-E5-05`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, Zink Tools, OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead 2026-10-18, and re-measured by the Lead on 2026-10-18 for a different reason than this row first gave. The route the title named ("one of them spawns a generator with the inherited environment") does not exist: none of the six bypassing suites spawns `configure.mjs` or `verify.mjs`, and the only one that names `configure.mjs` reads it as source text. What survives is a convention-consistency observation about `||=` seeding, which is not an F2 defect. (was: LATENT; the first refutation cited `buildConfig` call counts, a symbol that is not the route — see `Evidence`)
- Root cause: the shared test helper assigns the three router variables, which is the documented way to keep real values out of test lanes, while six suites pre-set the same variables with the nullish-assignment operator, preserving whatever the invoking host exported. The divergence is real but its stated route is not: none of the six runs a generator or calls a router resolver, so on today's tree the bypass keeps a host value inside the test process instead of carrying it into a generated fixture. Reaching the operator's real router endpoint would need a lane that both skips the helper and calls `resolveRouterBaseUrl()`/`resolveRouterApiKey()`; measured, no such lane exists in the six.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/helpers/router-env.mjs:21-36` — plain assignment of all three variables at `:27-29` and the restore closure at `:30-35` · Measured bypass population, six suites: `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs:22-24`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a1-stack-authority.test.mjs:14-16`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a2-stack-registry.test.mjs:24-26`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a3-queue-writer-lock.test.mjs:20-22`, `tests/m12-t2.2-k1-announce-discard-reason.test.mjs:17-19` and `tests/m10-t2-r14-review-defects.test.mjs:5-7` · Where the router values are actually read: three **exported** resolvers — `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1291` (`resolveRouterHost`), `:1298` (`resolveRouterBaseUrl`), `:1308` (`resolveRouterApiKey`) — each of which throws `ROUTER_*_UNAVAILABLE` when unset; `buildConfig` at `:788` only composes them, so counting `buildConfig` calls was the wrong probe · Generator consumers: `configure.mjs:1252` and `:1593` write `stableJson(buildSecretsPlan())`, `verify.mjs:311-312` and `:511` call the resolvers and compare the plan · Measured with `temp/f324-probe.mjs` over all 122 tracked suites: exactly six pre-set with `||=`, twelve import the helper, and of the six **none spawns a generator** — `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs` resolves the path at `:36` and reads its source text at `:38`, and its only child processes are `git archive` at `:98` and `tar` at `:104`, whose imported symbol `governancePluginFiles` at `:109-114` reads no `ZINK_ROUTER_*`; `tests/m10-t2-r14-review-defects.test.mjs:89` mentions `validateConfig` only inside a comment and imports `evaluateCapabilityAccess`/`AGENT_SPAWN_POLICY`, neither of which reads a router variable · Measured consequence: that suite prints tests 4, pass 3, fail 0, skipped 1 at baseline AND with a sentinel exported for all three variables, and the sentinel string appears 0 times in its output (`temp/f324-base.log`, `temp/f324-sent.log`).
- Reproduction: the measurement this row said it could not run was run: a sentinel exported for one of the three variables leaves the single suite at the same counts and never reaches the generated config, so the credential-into-fixture route is not reproducible today. The surviving measurement: assert at the head of each bypassing suite that the value it sees equals the documented placeholder, which is exactly what a host-exported router host would violate.
- Acceptance criteria: one policy for all suites — either every lane that can build the deployed config calls the helper that forces placeholders, or the suites that keep the preserving form assert that the values they see are the documented placeholders; no lane may write a real credential into a fixture or print one.
- Related findings: F-264, F-103, F-023 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E5 — remaining orphan files

### F-338 — the same `uv` is pinned at three places with two different versions, and no lane ever compares them

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-01`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Build & runtime config, CI workflow
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — escalated to the owner on 2026-10-20 (M12/T9): which uv version is canonical (the image and the Windows driver pin one, CI pins another). The agent stopped at the safest boundary (AGENTS.md §7); this row stays in the queue until the owner decides.
- Root cause: each `uv` pin was written by a different surface (image base, CI installer, Windows driver) and each is frozen by its own test, so nothing needs to agree with anything else. The consequence is that the `uv export … && cmp` equivalence check that proves `pdf-runtime/requirements.txt` equals the lock can be exercised by a different exporter than the one that produced the file, and every lane still reads green. · Evidence for "no lane compares": two tests name a version — `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:106` carries `uv==0.12.5` inside a fixture workflow string, and `tests/m3-t2-contract.test.mjs:29` regex-pins the Dockerfile line `ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:0.12.0@sha256:606e70c7…` as source text — and neither compares the image, CI and driver pins against each other.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:1` `FROM ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:0.12.0@sha256:606e…` · Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:62` `pip install "uv==0.12.5"` · Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t2-windows.ps1:7` `$uvVersion = "0.12.0"` · Where the pins are frozen individually: `tests/m3-t2-contract.test.mjs:29` pins the Dockerfile string as source text, `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:446-452` requires only that a `uv sync --locked` step precedes `npm run test:knowledge` · Where the equivalence is claimed: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:21-23` (`uv export` + `cmp`), consumed only by a manual `docker build`.
- Reproduction: static — read the three version strings and grep the lanes for any comparison between them; none exists. Mutation: advance CI's `uv` to 0.12.6 (or the image to 0.12.1) and confirm `npm run verify`, `repo:check` and CI stay green while the exporter that produced `requirements.txt` and the exporter that validates it are now different programs.
- Acceptance criteria: one place declares the `uv` version (or an explicit assertion compares image, CI and driver pins), and a test fails when any of the three diverges from the declared value.
- Related findings: F-306, F-218 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A — build/runtime config ngoài mẫu 300 file nguồn

### F-339 — the secret-scan allowlist suppresses 150 tracked paths by an unanchored regex and no policy check reads it

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-02`
- Affected modules: Build & runtime config, CI workflow, tests
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — allowlist scope and the absence of a reader both measured at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: the scan's suppression is expressed as an unanchored path regex rather than as line-precise fingerprints, and the repository policy gate validates the gitleaks Action pinning and flags but never opens the allowlist files. Growth of the suppressed set is therefore invisible to every lane. · Narrowed from the lane report on my own re-measure: this is scope hygiene, not an observed leak — the credential-shaped value the lane cited (`tests/helpers/router-env.mjs:19`, name only) is a self-evidently synthetic `test-router-key-not-a…` string.
- Evidence: Where: `.gitleaks.toml:6-9` `paths = [ '''tests/.*''', '''integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README\\.md''' ]` — the `tests/.*` entry is unanchored, which is why it also suppresses nested `tests/` directories · measured: `git ls-files` matched by the two patterns = **150** tracked paths — 126 under root `tests/`, 23 under nested `apps/knowledge/tests/` and `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/tests/` (including a vault fixture directory), and the one explicitly listed `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md` · Where: `.gitleaks.toml:8` also allowlists all of `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md`, which `.gitleaksignore:1` already fingerprints line-precisely (one of the two is redundant) · measured: `.gitleaksignore` carries **7** fingerprints, not the 2 the lane reported · Where the scan runs: `.github/workflows/security.yml:55` only · Where the policy gate stops: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:533-580` validates the action SHA, ordering and the two `GITLEAKS_*` flags and never reads `.gitleaks.toml` or `.gitleaksignore`.
- Reproduction: static — count the tracked paths the allowlist regex matches and diff that against the fingerprint file; mutation — commit a token-shaped string under any `*/tests/` path or add a secret-shaped line to the orchestration README and observe `secret-scan` still reports zero findings.
- Acceptance criteria: the allowlist is bounded (per-file fingerprints or an anchored list with a measured ceiling) and `npm run repo:check` fails when a tracked path enters the suppressed set without a recorded fingerprint naming it.
- Related findings: F-047 (scan scope, not allowlist scope) · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A — build/runtime config

### F-340 — `pdf-runtime/requirements.txt` is a hash-pinned pip artifact that cannot install two of its own pins

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-03`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Build & runtime config
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — escalated to the owner on 2026-10-20 (M12/T9): the requirements.txt export form: regenerate with --emit-index-url and re-pin the two digest readers, or declare an export-only listing. The agent stopped at the safest boundary (AGENTS.md §7); this row stays in the queue until the owner decides.
- Root cause: the export was written for a resolver that had the PyTorch index configured elsewhere, so the two `+cpu` distributions carry `--hash` lines while the file declares no index for them. Any consumer that treats the file as an install contract fails, and the only equivalence proof of the file lives inside a manual image build.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/requirements.txt:480` `torch==2.13.0+cpu` and `:489` `torchvision==0.28.0+cpu`, both followed by `--hash=sha256:…` · measured: **0** occurrences of `--index-url`, `--extra-index-url`, `--find-links` or `pytorch` in the file, against **4** occurrences of `https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu` in `pdf-runtime/uv.lock` (declared as the source) and `pdf-runtime/pyproject.toml:26-33` · Where it is consumed: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:21-23` (`uv export … && cmp`, manual build only) and two digest reads at `pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/manifest.py:35` and `apps/knowledge/tests/test_pdf_runtime.py:70` (the file is read) with `:88-89` (a `(?m)^<pkg>==[^\\s]+.*\\` regex over five package names), the latter asserting only that a hash-continued line exists for each name · Where the real install happens: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:24,32` (`uv sync --locked`).
- Reproduction: static — grep the file for an index directive; the pins that need one are the only ones without it. A confirming run is `pip install --require-hashes -r apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/requirements.txt`, which the read-only audit rule forbids, so the row stands on the file's own contents.
- Acceptance criteria: the artifact either carries the index it needs (and a lane installs from it), or it is renamed/declared as an export-only listing so no consumer can read it as an install contract.
- Related findings: F-306 (same produced-but-unconsumed shape, different mechanism), F-223 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A

### F-341 — the T5 acceptance nginx overlay defines the same location as `default.conf` without its three proxy timeouts

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-04`
- Affected modules: Infrastructure/docker, Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — corrected by the 2026-10-19 challenge pass: "no lane mounts either file" is false. `default.conf` IS mounted by the enforced core stack at `infrastructure/docker/compose/compose.core.yml:115-116`, while the overlay that replaces it at that same target is mounted only by the manual T5 acceptance compose, so the missing-timeout state exists in the tree and runs in no lane — the same reasoning that put `F-293` at LATENT.
- Root cause: the acceptance conf was written as a parallel copy of the production location block instead of a superseding fragment, so the directives that were added to `default.conf` later never reached it, and the two files are never compared. nginx's 60 s default then applies to the very run that exercises the long index/embed paths.
- Evidence: Where: `infrastructure/nginx/conf.d/default.conf:19-21` `proxy_connect_timeout 60s`, `proxy_read_timeout 300s`, `proxy_send_timeout 300s` on `location /knowledge/` · Where: `infrastructure/nginx/conf.d/t5-acceptance.conf:10-16` defines the same `location /knowledge/` with none of the three · Where the overlay wins: `infrastructure/docker/compose/compose.m3-t5.acceptance.yml:15-17` mounts `t5-acceptance.conf` at `/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf` · Where the pinning stops: `tests/m3-t1-contract.test.mjs:98-105` pins eight other directives of `default.conf` (not the timeouts) and `tests/m3-t5-contract.test.mjs:149-151` pins only the two `/ollama/api/*` locations · measured: repo-wide grep finds the three directives only in `default.conf` and a `scratch/` copy. · measured 2026-10-19 (challenge pass): the file the overlay replaces is itself mounted in the enforced core stack — `infrastructure/docker/compose/compose.core.yml:115` `source: ../../nginx/conf.d/default.conf` → `:116` `target: /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf` — so "no lane mounts either file" was wrong; what no lane mounts is `t5-acceptance.conf`.
- Reproduction: static — diff the two `location /knowledge/` blocks for the timeout directives. A confirming run is a manual T5 acceptance pass with an index/embed call exceeding 60 s, which shows as a 504 in that run only.
- Acceptance criteria: one source of truth for the `/knowledge/` proxy settings (a shared fragment, or a test that asserts the acceptance conf carries every directive the production conf declares).
- Related findings: F-323 (the same acceptance-pair drift shape in compose), F-223 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A

### F-342 — `.editorconfig` and `repo:check` prescribe opposite trailing-whitespace rules for Markdown

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-05`
- Affected modules: Build & runtime config, Governance docs
- Severity: Low
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead at HEAD: the gate already defers to the editor-facing `.editorconfig` — `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1147-1158` asserts exactly that, so the two authorities no longer prescribe opposite trailing-whitespace policy. (was: LATENT)
- Root cause: two authorities state opposite requirements for the same file class, and neither names the other. The editor-facing rule exists for Markdown's hard-break semantics; the gate-facing rule rejects the same bytes. Which one wins depends on which surface the operator touches last.
- Evidence: Where: `.editorconfig:18-19` `[*.md]` with `trim_trailing_whitespace = false` · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:707-712` — `checkWorkingTreeTextPolicy` fails any tracked file whose line ends `/[ \t]+\r?$/` · Where that runs: `npm run repo:check`, a `verify` stage, and the CI `quality-gate` · measured: count of tracked `.md` files whose lines end in whitespace = 0.
- Reproduction: static — add two trailing spaces to a `docs/*.md` line and run `npm run repo:check`; prettier preserves them per `package.json:21`'s markdown glob and `.editorconfig` declares them legal, while the policy check reddens.
- Acceptance criteria: one authority owns trailing-whitespace policy for Markdown and the other defers to it (either the gate exempts `*.md` hard breaks, or the editorconfig rule is removed and the docs are normalized).
- Related findings: F-129 (governance docs describing reality), F-153 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A

### F-344 — nine legacy ids cited by archived execution contracts resolve to no register row

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-OB-02`
- Affected modules: Governance docs, Architecture + contracts docs
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — measured 89 archive-cited ids, 9 unresolvable (measured against live rows ∪ §2 entries), at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: the register's own §0/§1 promise is one-directional. Rows keep a `Legacy register id` so a row can be traced to PLAN/archive, but nothing maps an archive id back to a row when the row was closed and removed, so the closure record and the archive drift apart silently. The lane report said 87 ids / 6 unresolved; my own re-measure with a broader id pattern found 89 and 9 — the lane said 6, my first pass said 7, and the 2026-10-19 challenge pass added `R3-P04-03` and `R4-V01-01`, which resolve against neither a live row nor a §2 entry once the table is read with its real predicate.
- Evidence: Where the claim lives: `docs/execution/findings.md` §1 ("Mỗi hàng giữ `Legacy register id` để `PLAN.md` §4.17/§11 và các contract archive vẫn tra được về id cũ") · Unresolvable ids and their archive pointers: `R6-V10-01` → `docs/contracts/execution/archive/2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R1.md:167`, `R6-V11-01` → same file `:202`, `R6-V03-01`/`R6-V03-02`/`R6-V04-04` → `2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R2.md:50,:135,:159,:607`, `R6-V05-01` → `2026-10-15-M12-T2.1-R3.md:243`, `R16-DRIVER-05` → `2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R2.md:614` and `2026-10-17-M12-T2.3-R1.md:282` · `R3-P04-03` → `2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R1.md:31` and `archive/README.md:9` · `R4-V01-01` → `2026-10-15-M12-T2.1-R3.md:229` and `2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R2.md:382` · measured: each of the 9 exists in `PLAN.md` (1–4 hits), so only archive→register breaks · measured: `grep -hoE 'R[0-9]+-[A-Z0-9]+-[0-9]+' docs/contracts/execution/archive/*.md | sort -u` = 89.
- Reproduction: static — extract the id set from the six archives and test membership against the register (both §1 rows and the §2 closure table); 9 fail.
- Acceptance criteria: every archive-cited id resolves either to a live row or to a §2 closure entry, and the §1 traceability sentence states the direction that is actually guaranteed.
- Related findings: F-161 (citations that dead-end in a deleted file), F-129 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit B

### F-348 — the routed closeout procedure deletes `ACTIVE.md` while the protocol it defers to forbids deleting before archiving

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R7-OC1-01`
- Affected modules: Governance docs, OpenClaw integration
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — three authorities re-read at HEAD 2026-10-19; the deletion order has not yet destroyed a record — the one loss on record was an overwrite (see Evidence)
- Root cause: the closeout sequence exists twice with different content. The protocol that `AGENTS.md:19` makes authoritative for the execution-contract lifecycle requires the contract to be copied into `archive/` before deletion; the skill that `AGENTS.md:157`/`:285` routes closeout work to deletes it as step 4 and never mentions archiving, and `AGENTS.md`'s own §3 closeout line repeats the deletion-only form. An agent that follows the routed procedure destroys the only durable record of what was authorized, and no gate can notice because the file it deletes is Git-ignored.
- Evidence: Where the prohibition lives: `docs/contracts/execution/README.md:89-90` "→ archive ACTIVE.md into docs/contracts/execution/archive/ and run its scrub rule → update PLAN.md / required durable docs, citing the archived file name" and `:100-101` "an archived contract is the only durable record of what was authorized, so closeout copies it first and deletes it second" · Where the deletion order lives: `.agents/skills/validating/SKILL.md:124` "4. delete `docs/contracts/execution/ACTIVE.md`;" (measured: the word `archive` occurs once in that file, at `:33`, in an unrelated list) · Where AGENTS.md repeats it: `AGENTS.md:86` "close the task by synchronizing `PLAN.md`, deleting `ACTIVE.md`, and leaving a clean working tree" · Where the practice is proven real: `docs/contracts/execution/archive/` holds 6 dated contracts · Nearest incident, with its recorded cause corrected 2026-10-19: `PLAN.md:6099-6100` says "it had already bitten once: R2's text was lost to an **overwrite** and recovered only from a session transcript" — the 2026-10-15 loss was an `ACTIVE.md` overwrite, not a delete-before-archive. This row's deletion order is the same class of hazard and has not yet fired; `F-161` records that the archive mechanism (owner decision `H10` / proposal `R3-P04-03`) now keeps a durable copy.
- Reproduction: static — `sed -n '114,131p' .agents/skills/validating/SKILL.md` next to `sed -n '85,104p' docs/contracts/execution/README.md` and `sed -n '84,88p' AGENTS.md`; the three cannot all be followed.
- Acceptance criteria: one closeout sequence exists; the skill and `AGENTS.md` either carry the archive step or cite `docs/contracts/execution/README.md` as the authority for it, and an assertion pins that a contract named in `PLAN.md` is present in `archive/` before its deletion is recorded.
- Related findings: F-161, F-178, F-129 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit C1 — governance docs and skills

### F-349 — the routed validation procedure demands "every required gate" while naming none, and the gate registry is unreachable from the authority chain

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps

The registry itself is real and gate-checked against `package.json`, but `AGENTS.md` never names it, its §1 responsibility map has no `docs/development/` row, and §5 has no route to it, so an agent can only find the gate list by already knowing it exists. Grading this Medium rather than High because the registry is enforced (drift is caught) — the defect is discoverability of the required set, not its absence.

- Acceptance criteria: the closeout/validation procedure states where the gate list lives (one line citing `docs/development/conventions.md`), and the route is added to the check that already exists: `checkGateRegistry` (`scripts/repository-policy.mjs:854-882`, measured, wired at `:956-959`) already fails when the registry and `package.json.scripts` diverge in either direction, so the residual demand is only that a governance file names the registry — not a new enforcement.

### F-350 — PLAN uses a `Task Status` value its own §2 vocabulary does not admit

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps

### F-351 — the Active Task block carries two task identities and two `Task Status` lines, so the authorization check has no single answer

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-OC1-04`
- Affected modules: Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — identity and status lines re-read at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: `AGENTS.md` §2 and the execution-contract protocol both require the ACTIVE contract's Task ID to equal the PLAN Active Task ID, but the PLAN block states two different identities and two statuses. Corrected 2026-10-19: `Task:` itself is single-valued (`PLAN.md:70`), so the disagreement is **cross-field** — `Task: M12/T4` versus `Active revision`/`Plan Revision: M12/T2.2-R3` (`:82`, `:335`) — plus two live `Task Status:` lines (`:235`, `:253`). The equality test is therefore not mechanically decidable from the header: whichever line a reader picks, another line contradicts it, and no machine reads the header at all (`grep -rn "Task Status" scripts/ tests/ Makefile` = **0**).
- Evidence: Where: `PLAN.md:69-70` "Milestone: M12 … / Task: M12/T4 — Governance-record integrity …" · Contradicted by: `PLAN.md:82` "Active revision: M12/T2.2-R3 — … materialized 2026-10-17" and `PLAN.md:335` "Plan Revision: M12/T2.2-R3" · Two statuses: `PLAN.md:235` "Task Status: IN PROGRESS — …" and `PLAN.md:253` "Task Status: **`M12/T2.2-R3` is CLOSED 2026-10-17.**" inside the same block, with `:270` recording a superseded status as history · Authority requiring the single answer: `AGENTS.md:40-45` (validity conditions for `ACTIVE.md`) and `docs/contracts/execution/README.md:29`. · measured 2026-10-19: the Active Task block is fenced from `PLAN.md:68` to `:605` (538 lines), so the header an agent must resolve before acting is not a short block, and nothing under `scripts/`, `tests/` or the `Makefile` parses `Task Status` or `Task:`.
- Reproduction: static — `sed -n '69,82p;235,253p;335p' PLAN.md`; the block yields two task ids and two statuses.
- Acceptance criteria: exactly one Task identity and one `Task Status` per Active Task block, with historical statuses marked as history outside the header, and a check that compares the header identity against the contract pointer.
- Related findings: F-089 (PLAN size makes the header hard to locate), F-350, F-161 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit C1

- Root cause: `README.md:24-27` publishes three module roots as the repository tree, and `AGENTS.md:242-243` refuses Zink Tools ownership of "orchestration, workflow state, or persistent Knowledge/Skill internals", so the tree that actually carries the durable workflow state belongs to no module an agent can route a change to. This is the ownership half of the same inventory gap `F-121` records for `platforms/`, and it is not covered by that row: `docs/architecture/zink-tools-boundaries.md:7` lists `integrations/openclaw/` as in scope, so a check built on `F-121`'s wording passes this tree.
- Evidence: Where the inventory lives: `README.md:24-27` — the tree block names only `apps/knowledge/`, `zink-skill-store/`, `zink-tools/` · Where ownership is denied: `AGENTS.md:242-243` "Zink Tools is a thin OpenClaw/n8n adapter-contract boundary. It does not own orchestration, workflow state, or persistent Knowledge/Skill internals." and `AGENTS.md` §9 "three logical modules only" · measured: `git ls-files integrations/ | wc -l` = **68** — 44 under `integrations/openclaw/topology/`, 13 under `integrations/openclaw/lib/`, 3 `plugins/`, 3 `templates/`, 2 `orchestration/`, 1 `verify.mjs` · Where the state lives: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs` (`findSession` defined at `:109`), `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs` (`withWriterLock` at `:481-484`), `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime.mjs` · Where a boundary doc does claim part of it: `docs/architecture/zink-tools-boundaries.md:7` names `integrations/openclaw/`, which contradicts the three-root tree rather than resolving it.
- Reproduction: static — `git ls-files integrations/ | wc -l`, then read `README.md:24-27` and `AGENTS.md:242-243`; no documented module owns the tree and `F-121`'s proposed top-level-directory gate would flag it as a second failure alongside `platforms/`.
- Acceptance criteria: `README.md`'s tree block and `AGENTS.md` §9 either name an owner for `integrations/` (a declared fourth module, or an explicit "hosting boundary for module X" with the state files exempted) or the 68 files move under a named module root, and the directory-ownership assertion from `F-121` passes for every tracked top-level directory.
- Related findings: F-121 (the `platforms/` half of the same inventory gap), F-343 (a normative doc with no implementation), F-129 · nguồn: R7-X correction pass — clause split out of the F-121 merge after the challenge lane showed that row's gate does not reach this tree

### F-361 — two ignored state files contradict the owner's recorded deployment and disposition decisions, and the bootstrap order puts them on the read path

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-IG-01`
- Affected modules: Working state files, Governance docs
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — both contradictions re-read at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: `.agents/states/` files are non-authoritative by `AGENTS.md` §6, yet four tracked procedures tell an agent to read `context.md` on a cold start, and the two files that carry current-task claims state the opposite of what `PLAN.md` records. The state was written for the M11/T3 band and was never refreshed or deleted after it closed, so a reader who follows the bootstrap order inherits a false statement about the live host and a false statement about what the owner excluded.
- Evidence: Where the state lies about the host: `.agents/states/context.md:22` "Live WSL gateway still runs the M11/T1-R2 build; M11/T3-R1 is NOT installed." · What the authority records: `PLAN.md:60` "**M11/T3-R1 is CLOSED 2026-10-13** (`REMOTE-BACKED`, **DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED** on the live WSL gateway)" and the same cell "This build is DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED on the live WSL gateway (owner grant 2026-10-13, ONE `--apply` …)" · measured: `grep -c "DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED" PLAN.md` = **9** · Where the state lies about evidence level: `.agents/states/context.md:12` "Evidence level is LOCAL-VERIFIED, not REMOTE-BACKED" against `PLAN.md:60` `REMOTE-BACKED` · Where the state re-labels an owner decision: `.agents/states/handoff.md:76` "## Deferred clusters the owner has not selected" and `:78-81` naming "per-document quarantine and the incremental-publish redesign", while `PLAN.md:60` lists those same items under "the owner-excluded items" · Where the read path is prescribed: `.agents/skills/execution/SKILL.md:91` "read `.agents/states/context.md` if present", `.agents/skills/debugging/SKILL.md:37` ".agents/states/context.md when present;", `.agents/skills/planning/SKILL.md:215`, `.agents/skills/validating/SKILL.md:125` · measured: `grep -rn 'states/context.md' AGENTS.md .agents/skills/*/SKILL.md | wc -l` = **4** · Why nothing catches it: the files are Git-ignored (`.gitignore:20 /temp/*` and the states rule), so no lane reads them, and `AGENTS.md` §6 says state must not override `PLAN.md` — the contradiction is only visible to a human who opens both.
- Reproduction: static — `sed -n '12p;22p' .agents/states/context.md` next to `sed -n '60p' PLAN.md`, and `sed -n '76,81p' .agents/states/handoff.md` next to the same cell; then `grep -rn 'states/context.md' AGENTS.md .agents/skills/*/SKILL.md`. A machine check is possible: an assertion that the state files' deployment and evidence-level claims match `PLAN.md`'s Active Task cell, which today fails.
- Acceptance criteria: no state file on the bootstrap read path states a deployment, evidence-level or owner-disposition fact that `PLAN.md` contradicts; either the stale M11/T3 files are refreshed/deleted per `.agents/states/README.md` and `validating/SKILL.md:125`, or the bootstrap steps say that state is historical and `PLAN.md` wins without comparison.
- Related findings: F-368 (the same files also fail the freshness-header protocol), F-161, F-355 · nguồn: R7-IG delta audit — the Git-ignored populations no earlier round opened

### F-362 — a tracked archive orders re-application of three held diffs whose content is already in the tree

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-IG-02`
- Affected modules: Working state files, Gates + CI, OpenClaw integration
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — landed content measured line-by-line at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: three M12/T2.2 items stopped before admission and preserved their diffs in `.agents/states/*-pending-admission.md`; the archived contract then tells the next band to re-apply from those files. Since they were written, the same work landed through other bands, so the instruction now points an executor at applying already-present code — and the only copy of the "still pending" signal lives in a Git-ignored file no lane reads.
- Evidence: Where the order lives: `docs/contracts/execution/archive/2026-10-16-M12-T2.2-R2.md:364` "`.agents/states/b8-pending-admission.md` for re-application under a grant", `:68` "the three options: `.agents/states/b16-pending-admission.md`", `:389` "preserved at `.agents/state…`" · Where the files declare themselves unapplied: `.agents/states/b9-pending-admission.md:1` "re-apply after `lib/schema.mjs` is admitted to the R2 P1 divergence allowlist", `.agents/states/b16-pending-admission.md:4` "Status: **BLOCKED — premise refuted, awaiting one owner line.** Working tree reverted", `:20` "`m8-t2.16:507` … **NOT granted**" · measured against the tracked tree, added lines of each held diff: b8 **87/88**, b9 **74/75**, b16 **93/104** appear verbatim · Where the one exception is itself landed: `package.json`'s `test` script already lists `tests/m8-t2.3-blocked-verdict.test.mjs`, `tests/m12-t2.2-b9-manifest-completeness.test.mjs` and `tests/m11-t1-r2-file-lock-asset-predecessor.test.mjs`, which are the suites the single unmatched `+` line adds · Where the code landed: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/native-boundary.mjs` carries `enterNativeRun` ×7, `gateNativeAnnounce` ×4, `finishNativeRun` ×4, `sanitizeNativePrompt` ×3, `registerNativeGuard` ×1 (measured by counting occurrences in the tracked file) · measured: `PLAN.md:60` records the boundary diagnostics as landed in `3cb8274` · Why no gate sees it: the three files are Git-ignored, so no lane can compare the order against the tree.
- Reproduction: static — extract every `+` line of the three held diffs and test it against the concatenation of the tracked tree (`temp/o1x-e3.mjs` does exactly this): 254 of 267 added lines are already present. Then read the archive's re-apply sentence. A machine check: a test asserting that a diff an archive instructs to re-apply is not already applied — today it fails for all three files.
- Acceptance criteria: an archived contract must not order re-application of work that is already in the tree — either the pending-admission files are deleted/annotated `ALREADY LANDED via <commit>` and the archive lines corrected, or the re-application instruction carries a check that the target diff is absent before it is offered to an executor.
- Related findings: F-364 (the tracked test comment that names the same B-16 work as ungranted), F-161, F-344 (citations into content the repository no longer carries) · nguồn: R7-IG delta audit — the Git-ignored populations no earlier round opened

### F-365 — three PostgreSQL archives sit under `backups/knowledge-t5/` with no tracked producer, one of them empty

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R7-IG-05`
- Affected modules: Infrastructure, Working state files
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — byte-level contents, producer names and guards measured at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: the tracked T5 driver writes `<backupRoot>/m4-recovery-<runId>.dump`, refuses a zero-length archive and checksums the file it produced, but the actual `backups/knowledge-t5/` population is named `knowledge.dump` inside per-run directories — a name no tracked file produces. Whatever wrote them was a different (manual or ad-hoc) path, so the repository holds three database dumps of the `knowledge` schema whose provenance, digest and expiry nothing defines, and the only pointer that ties a directory to a run lives in ignored scratch.
- Evidence: measured: `backups/knowledge-t5/` holds 4 run-id directories — `4d26bc4f820d427b85339aee2286c9b2/knowledge.dump` = **319 932 bytes**, magic `PGDM`; `a02b3919a8aa43de9ebc7c21cb5d285b/knowledge.dump` = **319 932 bytes**, magic `PGDM`; `fe69e04156204b739933733b3e703adc/knowledge.dump` = **0 bytes**; `0a4003be37b34fa589b36e67e298f537/` = empty · What the tracked driver writes instead: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t5-live.ps1:25` `$dumpPath = Join-Path $backupRoot "m4-recovery-$runId.dump"`, `:271-272` the container/temporary names, `:277` `pg_dump … --format=custom --no-owner --no-acl --schema=knowledge` · Where the driver refuses exactly what is on disk: `:294` `… (Get-Item $temporaryDump).Length -le 0) { throw "Recovery archive copy is empty" }`, `:280-286` inventory guards, `:301` `Assert-FileChecksum $dumpPath $dumpSha256` · measured: no tracked file contains the string `knowledge.dump` (`git grep -l knowledge.dump` exits 1) · Where the only run pointer lives: `temp/knowledge-t5/evidence/live-4d26bc4f….json` (6 701 B), `live-a02b3919….json` (3 602 B), `live-fe69e041….json` (3 174 B), `live-0a4003be….json` (6 324 B) — all Git-ignored · Why it is not merely untidy: `.gitignore:16 /backups/*` is root-anchored, so these bytes are excluded from review yet live inside the working tree the runtime loads.
- Reproduction: static — `ls -l backups/knowledge-t5/*/`, read the first four bytes of each `knowledge.dump`, then `git grep -l "knowledge.dump"` (no hits) and `sed -n '25p;294p' infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t5-live.ps1`. Gate: a policy check that every archive under `backups/` is produced by a tracked script and carries a recorded digest — today it fails for all three.
- Acceptance criteria: either the T5 driver owns `knowledge.dump` naming (producer tracked, digest recorded in `PLAN.md` §8, retention stated) or the three archives are removed by an owner-authorized deletion; no zero-length recovery archive remains in a path the repository calls a backup, and no run's only pointer lives in ignored scratch.
- Related findings: F-366 (the same directory has no owner or retention rule), F-220 (tracked junk that advertises content), F-355 · nguồn: R7-IG delta audit — the Git-ignored populations no earlier round opened

### F-366 — `backups/` holds the audit record's snapshots and database dumps with no tracked owner, retention rule, or ignore coverage for nested paths

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R7-IG-06`
- Affected modules: Repository hygiene, Governance docs
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — directory population, ignore rules and the citing set measured at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: the owner's audit protocol requires a copy of the register in `backups/` before every write, so the directory has accumulated 33 register snapshots plus a database-dump subtree, but no tracked document declares who owns it, what it is for, or when entries may be deleted, and `AGENTS.md` §10 only classifies backups as protected content. The ignore rule that hides it is root-anchored (`/backups/*`), so a nested directory of the same name is stageable, and the one rule that would prevent that is recorded elsewhere as an open defect.
- Evidence: measured: `backups/` has **36 entries** — 33 `findings.*.md` snapshots, `.gitkeep`, `PLAN.pre-bug-prune-20261017-164726.md`, and the `knowledge-t5/` subtree of `F-365` · measured: `git ls-files '*backups/*'` returns only `backups/.gitkeep`, so 35 of 36 entries are untracked · Where the ignore is anchored: `.gitignore:16` `/backups/*`, `:17` `!/backups/.gitkeep`, `:18` `/logs/*`, `:20` `/temp/*` · measured: `git check-ignore -v backups/x` → matched by `.gitignore:16`, while `git check-ignore -v apps/knowledge/backups/x` exits 1 (**not ignored**, therefore stageable) · Where the rule is only a protection clause: `AGENTS.md` §10 "Never expose, print, commit, or summarize real secrets, … backups, or ignored secret values" · measured: the only tracked files mentioning `backups/` are `docs/contracts/execution/archive/2026-10-15-M12-T2.1-R3.md` and this register; neither declares an owner or a retention rule · Where the anchoring half of this gap is already recorded: `F-191` (this file `:2571-2573`) — "`backups/`, `logs/`, `temp/` are all root-anchored", so a nested `…/backups/` stages into a commit; no row says the directory itself is an unowned region.
- Reproduction: static — `ls backups | wc -l`, `git ls-files '*backups/*'`, `git check-ignore -v backups/x` and `git check-ignore -v apps/knowledge/backups/x`; then `git grep -l 'backups/'` to show no document owns the directory.
- Acceptance criteria: one tracked document names `backups/`'s owner, purpose and retention (and says snapshots are never the canonical record); the ignore rule is broadened so a nested `backups/` directory is either covered or explicitly disallowed by a policy check.
- Related findings: F-365 (the dumps in this directory), F-367 (the register states recoverable only here), F-313, F-220 · nguồn: R7-IG delta audit — the Git-ignored populations no earlier round opened

### F-367 — the audit record is recoverable only from the unowned ignored snapshot directory for everything before 2026-10-17T22:57

- Reproduction: static — `git log --oneline -- docs/execution/findings.md | wc -l` (25) against `ls backups/findings.*.md | wc -l` (33), then `git log --diff-filter=A --format='%h %ad' -- docs/execution/findings.md` to show tracking begins mid-day 2026-10-17; any question about a row deleted before that commit is unanswerable from the repository alone.
- Acceptance criteria: the register's history is answerable from the repository: either the pre-tracking snapshots are moved into a tracked, retention-stated location, or §2 records the deleted rows' identities for the pre-`3325e5e` window, and one document names the canonical copy when the two disagree.

### F-368 — four of six state files carry none of the six required freshness keys, so the documented resume check cannot run and closeout never deleted them

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-IG-08`
- Affected modules: Working state files, Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — per-file header census and the closeout commit's file list measured at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: `.agents/states/README.md` requires every non-empty state file to open with a six-field freshness header, and the resume procedure's step 2 is "Compare HEAD/working tree with the state freshness header". Four of the six current state files have **none** of the six fields and one has a single prose line, so that step has no input; the closeout procedure's own step 5 (delete `context.md` and `handoff.md`) never ran for the band that wrote them, so the files still describe M11/T3 as current work five bands later — which is how `F-361` became possible.
- Evidence: Where the requirement lives: `.agents/states/README.md:40` "## Required freshness header", `:44-49` the six fields `Task`, `Plan revision/state`, `Repository HEAD`, `Working tree`, `Last refreshed`, `Writer` · Where the check is prescribed: `.agents/states/README.md:128-129` "1. Re-read `AGENTS.md` and current `PLAN.md`. 2. Compare HEAD/working tree with the state freshness header." · Where deletion is prescribed: `.agents/skills/validating/SKILL.md:125` "5. delete transient `.agents/states/context.md` and `handoff.md` when present — never `docs/execution/findings.md`…" · measured, keys present in the first 14 lines of each file: `.agents/states/b8-pending-admission.md` **0/6** (it opens directly with a diff), `b9-pending-admission.md` **0/6**, `b16-pending-admission.md` **0/6** (it has `Item:`/`Status:` but none of the six), `wave-b-premises.md` **0/6**, `handoff.md` **1/6** and that one is a prose sentence ("Working tree clean."), `context.md` **4/6** (no `Working tree`, no `Last refreshed`) · measured: the M11/T3 closeout commit `f55d292` touches only `CHANGELOG.md`, `PLAN.md`, `README.md` — no `.agents/states/` path, so step 5 never ran · Where a parallel queue is forbidden: `.agents/states/README.md:35-38` "The durable defect register is **not** a state file … must not hold a parallel copy of the queue" and `:76` "Do not keep a parallel queue here, and do not re-grade" · measured on `wave-b-premises.md`: its premise identifiers are **19** contract item ids in section form (`R1-A12`, `R4-V01`, …), of which **0** match a live row or a §2 closure entry, so the file records verdicts about register items that this register does not carry — and the lane's own header at `:3-4` disclaims authority ("Not authority — the contract is").
- Reproduction: static — read the first 14 lines of each file in `.agents/states/` against `README.md:44-49` (4 files match zero of six), then `git show --stat f55d292` to show the closeout never deleted them. Gate: a policy assertion that every state file in the directory carries the six required keys and that no state file names a register item id without a corresponding row — today it fails on five of six files.
- Acceptance criteria: every non-empty state file carries the six-field freshness header so `README.md:129` can run, closeout deletes the band's `context.md`/`handoff.md` (or the protocol says who keeps them), and no state file records verdicts on register ids that the register does not contain.
- Related findings: F-361 (the false claims these files still carry), F-161, F-355 (governance files outside every enforced lane) · nguồn: R7-IG delta audit — the Git-ignored populations no earlier round opened

### F-369 — the runtime the gateway loads is six days behind HEAD and no lane compares them, so the state-root guard closed in the repo is absent from the tree that still mints nested ledger roots

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R8-M1-01`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw integration, Gates + CI
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — both sides measured at the live host and at HEAD 2026-10-19
- Root cause: the only proof the repository accepts for "this is what is running" is a digest ceremony over the packaged tree it just wrote, and no lane ever diffs the repo HEAD against the tree the gateway actually loads. Every fix that lands after the last operator apply is therefore recorded in the repo as closed while the governed runtime keeps executing the older code, and the residue that motivated the fix stays on disk.
- Evidence: Repo side: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs` is 23990 bytes, sha256 prefix `131cfc8d004bec3c`, and `grep -c LEDGER_ROOT_ALREADY_JOINED` = 1; `git log -S LEDGER_ROOT_ALREADY_JOINED --format=%aI` on that file returns `2026-10-16T09:49:28+07:00` (commit `bca4ad6`, "refuse a state root that is already a ledger root"). Live side: `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/extensions/zink-governance/lib/ledger.mjs` is 20914 bytes, sha256 prefix `c984cda8e0ab40fb`, mtime 2026-10-13T17:05Z, and the same grep = **0** — the guard is not in the code the gateway executes; `index.mjs` in the same tree is 335797 bytes, sha256 prefix `513beb15c3d7624c`, same mtime, so the whole tree is one deployment behind by three days of audit-fix commits. The artifact the guard was written to refuse is still on the live host: `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/operator/m7-t2/ledger/operator/m7-t2/ledger/` (mode 700, `events.jsonl` 0 bytes, `snapshot.json` 196 bytes carrying `governance_version, ingress_ids, last_event_digest, schema_version, sequence, tasks, updated_at` with `sequence=0`, `tasks={}`, `ingress_ids=[]`, `last_event_digest=null`, mtime 2026-09-25T03:32Z) — a second, empty, permission-valid governance root nested inside the real one, created 18 days before the guard and never cleaned. Contrast with the real root, measured intact in the same pass: `events.jsonl` 146220 bytes, 144 lines, 0 unparsable, `sequence` 1..144 with 0 duplicates and 0 gaps, `snapshot.json` `sequence=144` equal to the journal maximum. A third empty root sits beside it: `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/governance/ledger` (`events.jsonl` 0 bytes, `snapshot.json` `sequence=0`, mtime 2026-09-18T14:50Z) — no emitter was attributed to that one in this pass.
- Reproduction: read-only, two commands: `grep -c LEDGER_ROOT_ALREADY_JOINED integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs` (1) against the same grep on the deployed file (0), then `git log -S` for the landing date against `stat -c %y` of the deployed file. Why no lane catches it: `configure.mjs` and `verify.mjs` compare the packaged digests they just wrote, never the repo source those packages were built from, so a green verify is compatible with the deployed tree lacking any commit made after the last apply — and `F-086` shows the two byte-equal post-checks do not even agree on their own denominator.
- Acceptance criteria: one gate asserts that the tree the gateway loads corresponds to a named repo revision (either by diffing every loaded file against its repo source, or by recording the installed commit and asserting it is an ancestor of HEAD), and the pre-apply ceremony refuses to leave a nested empty ledger root inside the real one.
- Related findings: F-022 (the working-tree mount path that makes packaged digests a weaker proof than they look), F-086 (two byte-equal deployment post-checks with different denominators), F-147 (restore quarantines instead of restoring), F-275 · nguồn: R8-M1 live-runtime measurement

### F-370 — two quarantined copies of the governance plugin stay inside the live discovery path carrying the same manifest id, so the correct governed code survives only by config precedence

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R8-M1-02`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw integration, OpenClaw runtime libs
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — collision, digests and live load diagnostics all measured at the host 2026-10-19
- Root cause: a rollback leaves the displaced tree inside the very directory the plugin loader scans, and nothing owns that residue: the naming convention on disk (`-pre-mig-YYYYMMDD`, `-pre-r4-YYYYMMDD`) occurs in no tracked file, while the repository's own quarantine destination is a different path (`quarantined-t2`, `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:301`). Because a quarantined copy keeps its `openclaw.plugin.json` with the same `id`, the loader registers it as a competing candidate for the governed plugin, and the copy that executes is decided by origin precedence rather than by the deployment record.
- Evidence: Measured: `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/extensions/` (mode 700) holds exactly three directories, and each one's `openclaw.plugin.json` declares `id="zink-governance"` with `name="Zink Governance"` and the manifest keys `activation, configSchema, contracts, description, id, name`. The three payloads differ: active `zink-governance/index.mjs` 335797 bytes sha256 prefix `513beb15c3d7624c` (2026-10-13); `zink-governance-pre-mig-20260928/index.mjs` 147911 bytes sha256 prefix `e818183dd369f8e0` (2026-09-28); `zink-governance-pre-r4-20260928/index.mjs` 139374 bytes sha256 prefix `87d44882dc425e98` (2026-09-26). The two stale trees are byte-identical to each other in `lib/ledger.mjs` (15325 bytes, sha256 prefix `50efeec642032400`) and neither contains the state-root guard. Host behaviour: `~/.local/lib/node_modules/openclaw/dist/manifest-registry-*.js:673` pushes a `level: "warn"` diagnostic for a duplicate `manifest.id` and resolves a winner from `candidateWins`/origin, with the message text "duplicate plugin id resolved by explicit config-selected plugin; … will be overridden by config plugin". Live journal: `journalctl --user -u openclaw-gateway-zink.service | grep -c 'duplicate plugin id'` = **167**, all of the config-resolved variant and 0 of the plain "duplicate plugin id detected" variant, and the named winner is `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/extensions/zink-governance/index.mjs`. Repo side: `git grep -E "pre-mig|pre-r4"` returns 0 hits, so no tracked procedure can account for either directory.
- Reproduction: read-only: print the `id` field of every `~/.openclaw-zink/extensions/*/openclaw.plugin.json` (three identical ids) and count the warning lines in the unit's journal. Which copy would execute if the config selection were removed is not proven — flipping that selection is a live-runtime mutation, so it was not run in this pass.
- Acceptance criteria: the deploy and rollback ceremonies move a displaced tree outside every scanned directory (or rename its manifest id) as one transaction, a gate asserts exactly one manifest per plugin id under the extensions root, and the quarantine convention the live host actually uses is named in a runbook.
- Related findings: F-369 (the same deployment record that does not describe this tree), F-147 (restore quarantines instead of restoring), F-022 · nguồn: R8-M1 live-runtime measurement

### F-371 — the canonical delegation id is a 64-hex digest while the published envelope and the guard both require a UUIDv4, so every delegated tool call is refused with AUTH_DELEGATION_NOT_FOUND before it can reach a specialist

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: — (new in M12/T9; no legacy id)
- Affected modules: OpenClaw runtime libs, OpenClaw integration, Zink Tools
- Severity: Critical
- Status: OPEN — found while fixing another row in the M12/T9 band; filed here per §0.2.8 and not fixed in passing
- Root cause: the id a delegation is filed under is derived by one module and checked by another: `deriveDelegationId` (`integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/canonical.mjs`) returns `canonicalHash("zink-delegation-id-v1", …)` — a sha256 hex digest — while the published envelope `zink-tools/contracts/tool-call.schema.json` `$defs/delegated_provenance.delegation_id` is `{"$ref": "#/$defs/uuid_v4"}` and `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1820-1823` asserts `isUuidV4(delId)` before it resolves the delegation. A 64-hex string satisfies neither, so the check can never pass.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/canonical.mjs` `deriveDelegationId` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1818-1823` — `const delId = del.delegation_id ?? del.id; assert(typeof delId === "string" && isUuidV4(delId), "AUTH_DELEGATION_NOT_FOUND")` · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tool-call.schema.json` `$defs/delegated_provenance.delegation_id` · Evidence: the ledger keys `task.delegations[payload.delegation_id]` (`topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1349`, `:2257`) by the id the canonical builder produced, so the ledger and the guard disagree about the same value.
- Reproduction: Static proof chain: `canonicalHash` returns `createHash("sha256").update(...).digest("hex")` (64 hex characters, no dashes); `isUuidV4` tests `/^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-4[0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i`; no 64-hex string matches it. Executed by the plugin agent's F-242 lane, which asserts the refusal honestly instead of papering over it.
- Acceptance criteria: a delegation that the canonical builder produced is accepted by the guard and validates against the published envelope: the id is still derived deterministically from the same inputs, but in the form the contract and the guard already require, and a lane proves that a real delegation resolves instead of being refused.
- Related findings: F-242 (the live governed tool-call seam that surfaced it), F-157 (the approval-scope digest family), F-249 (the same class of contract-versus-reducer disagreement) · nguồn: M12/T9 band 3, found while fixing F-242

### F-372 — `applications/` is a third untracked, ownerless top-level tree — two empty directories that no canonical document names and no tracked file occupies

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: — (new in M12/T9; no legacy id)
- Affected modules: Legacy top-level tree, Repository hygiene
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — found while fixing another row in the M12/T9 band; filed here per §0.2.8 and not fixed in passing
- Root cause: the repository has three logical modules and a set of legacy trees; `models/` and `shared/` were the two ownerless top-level trees the F-137 gate found, and they were moved aside. `applications/` — `applications/document-manager` and `applications/knowledge-platform-web` — is a third one, still on disk, empty, untracked, and named by no architecture document.
- Evidence: Where: `applications/document-manager/` and `applications/knowledge-platform-web/` (both empty) · Where: `docs/architecture/repository-topology.md` — the owner map for every tracked top-level tree, which names `applications/` nowhere · Evidence: `git ls-files applications/` returns 0 paths; the F-137 gate (`tests/repository-policy.test.mjs`) refuses a top-level tree whose name or child names duplicate a Zink Tools capability path, and `applications/` does not trip it — so nothing in the tree refuses this one.
- Reproduction: Static: `git ls-files applications/` is empty and `Get-ChildItem applications -Recurse -Force` lists two directories and no file. The F-137 gate's own rule (any new top-level tree whose name or child names duplicate a `zink-tools/` capability path) does not fire for `applications/`, so the cleanliness the row names is not enforced anywhere.
- Acceptance criteria: the working tree has no untracked, ownerless top-level tree: `applications/` is either moved aside with the other legacy scaffolding (`models/`, `shared/`, which now live under `scratch/legacy-empty-trees/`) or named with an owner in `docs/architecture/repository-topology.md`, and a lane asserts that the set of top-level trees on disk matches the set the owner map names.
- Related findings: F-121 (every top-level module path maps to a documented owner), F-137 (the ownerless-tree gate) · nguồn: M12/T9 band 3, found while fixing F-137
